## Phase 1 — Foundation prototypes
Prioritize:
001 TUXEDO, COLUMN, STRUCTURE SHIRT, FOUNDATION TEE, DISTRICT DENIM, RUIN, MONUMENT, DOSSIER, RABBIT, SEAL, HOUSE CUFFLINKS, HOUSE STUDS, OPERATIVE, AFTER THE RAIN, 12 CM BOW, VAULT.

## Phase 2 — Fit and engineering
- develop blocks
- prototype
- fit
- revise
- golden sample
- tolerance sheet

## Phase 3 — Supply chain
- identify material suppliers
- identify hardware maker
- identify footwear maker
- identify leather maker
- identify watch development partner
- identify fragrance development/manufacturing partner
- document minimum order quantities and lead times

## Phase 4 — House systems
- packaging prototypes
- provenance system
- TUX GEN records
- website architecture
- recommendation graph
- content library

## Phase 5 — Launch preparation
- costing
- pricing
- labeling
- safety/regulatory review
- photography
- copy
- inventory
- fulfillment
- customer care

## Phase 6 — Release
Foundation → Second Movement → Third Movement.

## Phase 7 — Archive and iteration
Every released object enters the archive. Changes become new versions, not silent substitutions.

---

# 15. COSTING / PRICING WORKFLOW

For every product:

**MATERIAL COST + COMPONENT COST + LABOR + DEVELOPMENT AMORTIZATION + PACKAGING + LOGISTICS + QC + OVERHEAD ALLOCATION = LANDED COST**

Then establish target gross margin and channel economics.

Pricing must reflect:
- craft
- scarcity
- material quality
- development complexity
- aftercare
- provenance
- brand position

Never price solely from competitor imitation.

---

# 16. QC MASTER GATE

A product fails release if any of the following is unresolved:

- unsafe construction
- unacceptable material performance
- dimensional instability
- inconsistent hardware finish
- poor fit
- weak closure/fastener performance
- packaging damage risk
- missing care instructions
- incomplete provenance
- incorrect labeling
- unresolved supplier quality
- unresolved regulatory requirement
- unacceptable visual deviation from golden sample

Golden sample is the visual and construction reference.

---

# 17. HOUSE-LEVEL FINAL CHECK

Before any collection launch ask:

### Identity
Does it feel unmistakably EDRIS without the logo?

### Function
Does it solve a real problem?

### Beauty
Is it beautiful before the story is explained?

### Strangeness
Is there a second-glance detail?

### Meaning
Does the object carry a deeper house idea?

### Iranian soul
Is Iran present in the thinking rather than costume?

### Longevity
Would it still make sense in twenty years?

### System
Does it connect naturally to another EDRIS object?

### Craft
Can it be made properly?

### Archive
Will it become more interesting with age?

If the answer is no, redesign or delete.

---

# 18. MASTER LAUNCH PRIORITY

### Tier A — Build first
001 TUXEDO; STRUCTURE SHIRT; COLUMN; MONUMENT; DOSSIER; RABBIT; SEAL; HOUSE CUFFLINKS; HOUSE STUDS; OPERATIVE; AFTER THE RAIN; VAULT; 12 CM BOW.

### Tier B — Complete the wardrobe
FOUNDATION TEE; DISTRICT DENIM; RUIN; HEIRLOOM KNIT; AFTERHOURS; SPIRE; DECO; TWENTY-FOUR; NOIR; VOYAGE; AXIS; CREST; MIDNIGHT; ARCHIVE.

### Tier C — Expand the world
PATRON; MONSOON; PORTAL; WILD; OBSIDIAN; FRONTIER; RAIN; RELIC; VECTOR; NOCTURNE; SALON; HOUSE; LINK; BASTION; MASK; RELIQUARY; OBSERVATORY; ALTAR; WET STONE.

### Conditional / later
OUTPOST; ORACLE; FIELD; RAIN ON STONE; GROOMING; BAR; DESK.

---

# 19. WHAT IS NOW FINISHED

The following are considered conceptually finished:

- House identity
- House philosophy
- Product universe
- Product hierarchy
- Category coverage
- Product-family system
- Recommendation philosophy
- Client journey
- TUX GEN role
- Provenance philosophy
- Packaging architecture
- Atelier identity
- Digital information architecture
- Content architecture
- Launch hierarchy
- Quality gate
- Future-product discipline
- 001 design direction

# 20. WHAT IS NOW THE WORK

The remaining work is physical and operational:

1. Draw production patterns.
2. Produce prototypes.
3. Fit and revise.
4. Approve golden samples.
5. Engineer materials and hardware.
6. Establish suppliers.
7. Cost and price.
8. Complete regulatory/labeling review.
9. Build packaging.
10. Build TUX GEN records.
11. Build website/app data model.
12. Photograph and write the products.
13. Launch.
14. Repair, archive and iterate.

This is no longer a question of inventing more EDRIS categories.

It is the transition from **house design to house construction**.

---

# FINAL HOUSE STATEMENT

**EDRIS is a complete house.**

It does not need endless products to feel complete.
It needs excellent products, connected intelligently, made correctly, documented permanently and allowed to age.

A tuxedo leads to a shirt.
A shirt leads to a suit.
A suit leads to a watch.
A watch leads to a leather object.
A leather object leads to travel.
Travel leads to fragrance.
Fragrance leads to memory.
Memory becomes archive.
Archive becomes legacy.

**BUILD AGAINST TIME.**


---

# SOURCE FILE: _EXPANDED_ARCHIVES/044_USER_DATA_DUMP_AND_PROJECT_FILES/USER_DATA_DUMP.md

# USER DATA DUMP — EDRIS / PERSONAL PROJECTS

**Prepared:** 2026-10-15  
**Purpose:** Portable summary of user-specific facts, preferences, project decisions, and project files available to the assistant in its current saved context and Library.

## Important scope and limitations
This is a best-effort export of the user-specific information currently available to the assistant, not a complete account-level archive of every ChatGPT conversation, hidden system record, or every message ever sent. The assistant cannot independently retrieve every chat in the account as a single transcript. Some details below are explicit user-provided facts; others are summaries of prior project work. Where prior decisions conflict, the latest known canonical decision is preferred and older variants are identified as historical. The included files are existing Library artifacts that were accessible and materialized for this export; not every historical artifact is included.

---

# 1. Personal background and preferences

## Identity / broad background
- Name used for the fashion-house founder identity: **EDRIS NAZPARVAR**.
- User has described themself as an **Iranian Turk born in Mazandaran**.
- A cultural/geographic imagery path mentioned for the house: **Qaemshahr → Tehran → Paris → Rome → Los Angeles → New York**.
- A prior conversation stated the user was 26 in 2023; this is a historical statement, not a verified current age.

## Personality / creative tendencies inferred from conversations (not a diagnosis)
- Highly imaginative, ambitious, systems-oriented, and interested in turning ideas into real, usable artifacts.
- Strong preference for coherent worlds and unified design languages rather than isolated products.
- Interested in both aesthetics and engineering: tailoring, product systems, vehicles, mechanical systems, digital tools, commercialization, and technical research.
- Likes cinematic, dark, architectural, carefully engineered, distinctive designs; values originality and long-term identity.
- Wants practical deliverables (patterns, technical specifications, PDFs, app prototypes, licensing plans) rather than only high-level discussion.
- Iterative and exacting: frequently refines specifications and expects previous decisions to carry forward.
- Strongly dislikes visual sloppiness, interwoven/overlapping pattern pieces, formatting defects, curved lapel lines, sagging/oversized proportions, and generic or costume-like design.
- Wants design to feel fashionable and functional, not merely decorative.
- These are conversational inferences and may not capture the whole person.

---

# 2. Project: EDRIS — House of Fashion

## Canonical identity
- Public house: **EDRIS**.
- Descriptor: **A HOUSE OF FASHION — IRAN**.
- House line: **BUILD AGAINST TIME.**
- Founder / creative identity: **EDRIS NAZPARVAR**.
- Crest: **THE MASKED RABBIT**.
- Internal bespoke / atelier system: **TUX GEN**. TUX GEN is not intended as the public-facing house/sub-brand.

## Philosophy and DNA
Core thought: **“We civilize the wild. We build against time. We know everything eventually returns to nature. So we make something worth leaving behind.”**

Master idea: civilization against nature, created in the face of mortality. Nature → life → desire → civilization → creation → decay → death; death completes the cycle.

Permanent pillars:
1. **Ancient Soul — Iran:** Iranian history, architecture, gardens, poetry, geometry, landscapes, metalwork and textiles translated subtly into design; Iran is the soul, not costume.
2. **Dark Architecture:** Art Deco geometry as structural grammar; Gothic/ruin atmosphere, monumentality, melancholy and mortality without fantasy costume.
3. **Cinematic Glamour:** Old Hollywood, James Bond precision, Godfather authority, Grand Budapest Hotel theatrical discipline, strong film-language detail.
4. **The Feral Human:** instinct beneath civilization, sensuality, severity, danger, nature without literal animal costume.
5. **The Machine:** functional/engineered detail, mechanical beauty, technology versus humanity.
6. **Mortality:** time, ruins, memory, destiny, death, inheritance, artifacts and legacy.

Influences discussed: Dark Souls mood; BioShock; Metal Gear Solid 4; Watch Dogs; Cyberpunk; steampunk as a restrained accent; Grand Budapest Hotel; James Bond; Vito Corleone; Bruce Wayne; Agent 47; Donnie Darko’s rabbit; HoZho mask concept. Art Deco / 1920s–30s geometry and Old Hollywood masculinity are foundational. Futurism, tactical engineering and cyberpunk are accents, not costume.

Design test: beautiful at first glance, strange at second, meaningful at third; recognizable without the logo; Iranian in soul, architectural and materially convincing, durable enough to become an artifact, useful or emotionally necessary.

## Product universe / catalogue
House universe covers formal and casual clothing, bespoke tailoring, outerwear, footwear, leather goods, eyewear, jewelry, fragrance, accessories, watches/horology, grooming, future home/desk objects, packaging, atelier services, provenance, archive, quality standards and digital experiences.

The catalogue evolved through versions 1.0, 2.0, 2.1, 3.0, ecosystem master v4.2 and execution master v5.0. **EDRIS House Catalogue v3.0** is a canonical product/house master in the available files; **Execution Master v5.0** is the execution-layer document. Older catalogue versions are retained as historical drafts.

Product naming examples from the latest known house system include:
- **001 — THE ONE OF ONE TUXEDO**
- **THE OPERATIVE** — 001 watch concept
- **AFTER THE RAIN** — 001 fragrance concept
- **THE MASKED RABBIT** — house crest
- Product names include STRUCTURE SHIRT, COLUMN, MONUMENT, DOSSIER, RABBIT, SEAL, HOUSE CUFFLINKS, HOUSE STUDS, OPERATIVE, VAULT, 12 CM BOW, FOUNDATION TEE, DISTRICT DENIM, RUIN, HEIRLOOM KNIT, AFTERHOURS, SPIRE, DECO, TWENTY-FOUR, NOIR, VOYAGE, AXIS, CREST, MIDNIGHT, ARCHIVE, PATRON, MONSOON, PORTAL, WILD, OBSIDIAN, FRONTIER, RAIN, RELIC, VECTOR, NOCTURNE, SALON, LINK, BASTION, MASK, RELIQUARY, OBSERVATORY, ALTAR and WET STONE.

## Style / materials
- Dark, architectural, cinematic luxury; near-black/deep forest black, ivory, aged silver and restrained oxblood.
- Art Deco geometry and symmetry, machine-age luxury, gothic/ruin mood, Old Hollywood masculinity.
- Avoid generic “Persian luxury,” Persian costume, costume Gothic, medieval fantasy, literal steampunk/cyberpunk cosplay, military cosplay, logo-covered luxury, fake aristocracy, generic quiet luxury, excessive skulls/gears/claws and cheap trend-chasing.
- Jewelry language: aged/polished silver; Roman/architectural luxury (Bvlgari), Cartier-like refinement/status, Chrome Hearts-like substantial silver/gothic rebel craft; selective gold and dark metal.
- Watch references: Seiko SKX007, Rolex Submariner, Vacheron Constantin; goal is a James Bond-like tactical-dress watch direction.
- Sneaker references now part of DNA: Adidas Originals Adi2000, Niteball II and Yeezy 700.

## Website / app ecosystem
- Website/app should function as an ecosystem, not just a shop.
- Proposed areas: HOUSE (editorial/campaigns/films), SHOP (controlled discovery), PASSPORT (authenticated object records), ATELIER (consultation, measurements, project status, fittings, approvals, messages, repairs), ARCHIVE (private owner archive plus public approved archive).
- Digital passport fields may include piece ID, category, collection, creation date, materials, hardware, construction notes, pattern/version, atelier/craftsperson where appropriate, fitting/alteration history, authenticity/provenance with consent, care, photography, design notes and service history.
- TUX GEN modules: client profile, measurements/body scan data, design concepts, garment configuration, material/hardware library, pattern records, fitting records, alterations, production workflow, QC, digital passport, provenance and archive.
- Bespoke flow: consultation → measurement → design brief → material selection → configuration → pattern → toile/prototype → fitting → correction → final construction → QC → delivery → passport → archive → aftercare.
- Privacy principles: minimum necessary personal data, access control, versioned patterns, timestamped measurements, revision history, production data separated from unnecessary personal data, documented corrections.
- Intended bilingual Persian/English PWA/app. Earlier desired onboarding: up to 6 main questions before body scan plus 6 optional styling questions after scan; quick mode using user as default fit and bespoke mode; body scanning, visual models, textile suggestions by budget, tailoring manual/sketches and interactive modern UI.
- Product concept must not overclaim technical capability: production-grade AI body scanning, accurate 3D cloth simulation, validated 1:1 pattern generation and production infrastructure require separate validated engineering.

## Commercial/operational direction
- Move from concept invention to physical/operational execution: production patterns, prototypes, fitting and revision, golden samples, material/hardware engineering, suppliers, costing/pricing, regulatory/labeling review, packaging, TUX GEN records, website/app data model, product photography/copy and launch.
- No manipulative luxury tactics such as fake scarcity, countdowns or fabricated waitlists.
- Quality gate includes safe construction, material performance, dimensional stability, hardware consistency, fit, closures, packaging, care instructions, provenance, labeling, supplier quality, regulatory review and conformity to approved golden sample.

---

# 3. Project: TUXEDO 001 — ONE OF ONE

## Design direction (latest canonical spec)
- House: EDRIS; older files still use “Uncle Ed’s” as a historical working name.
- Identity: **001 / ONE OF ONE**.
- Midnight / forest-black luxury wool; very dark green undertone only if extremely subtle, with plain black also considered.
- Single-breasted, one button, long modern Hollywood/Savile Row body.
- Sculpted but not exaggerated Hollywood shoulder; structured shoulder and sleeve head; high armhole; full canvas; horsehair chest.
- Controlled waist; clean extended body; boxy/architectural rather than sagging or oversized.
- **12 cm straight peak lapel — maximum and locked preference.** User strongly dislikes a visibly curved lapel line; straight controlled outer edge, straight/controlled inner edge, sharp symmetrical peak, no decorative “belly,” no exaggerated roll.
- Deep black satin lapel facing; satin treatment considered for formal contrast. Later execution notes include satin facing.
- Double-jetted pockets; double vents.
- White fine formal cotton shirt, formal collar, French cuffs, clean/concealed front, aged-silver shirt studs.
- Large architectural black butterfly bow tie.
- Aged-silver jacket button and cufflinks.
- White boutonniere / lapel rose and white pocket square discussed.
- High-rise flat-front trousers, side adjusters, no belt loops. Historical specs conflict on satin trouser stripe: an earlier final master includes a black satin stripe; a prior user decision explicitly said no matching trouser stripe. Treat this as an unresolved version conflict that should be confirmed before production rather than silently assuming.
- Hidden Iranian geometry; black/deep-forest cupro/Bemberg lining with restrained oxblood interior accents considered.
- Approximate wool target in an earlier specification: 280–320 g/m², low-to-medium controlled sheen, good drape and structure.

## Body and pattern information (user-provided / historical)
Drafting base height: **180 cm** (user once said perhaps 181, but asked to use 180). Desired wearable range approximately 180–185 cm and 80–90 kg; user said current around 90 kg, wants suit to look best around 85 kg and still wearable at 80 and 90 kg. User wants a slimmer appearance; chest is larger; shoulders/biceps may increase; target posture is corrected/ideal posture. User wants to avoid later alterations and accommodate a range of body weights, but a real toile/fitting remains necessary.

Body measurements supplied (cm):
- Chest 105
- Waist 101
- Shoulder width 46
- Back length 80
- Sleeve 75
- Bicep 35
- Hips/seat 112
- Thigh 60
- Inseam 70
- Neck 45

Later desired/leaner draft values discussed:
- Chest 110
- Shoulder 50
- Bicep 40
- Seat 110
- Thigh 60
- Neck 45
- Minimum waist 90

Additional measurements supplied (cm):
- Elbow 35
- Shoulder to elbow 40
- Front rise 38
- Back rise 40
- Calf circumference 43
- Ankle circumference 26
- Outseam 100
- Pants worn to the left.

A separate raw tailor chart was also supplied in a later conversation: 49 cm, 54 cm, 30 cm, 56 cm, 105 cm, 73 cm, 35 cm, 18 cm, and 70 cm shoulder-to-wrist. The meanings of all entries were not fully mapped in the stored summary; do not treat these as finished-garment measurements without reconciling the tailor's chart.

Historical trouser spec included finished seat target 116–119 cm, thigh 64–66 cm, knee 45–46 cm, calf/ankle body references 43/26 cm, outseam reference 100 cm and a controlled slight break. Earlier spec says no stripe; later execution master includes stripe. Resolve before cutting.

## Pattern / tailoring caveats
- User reported previous generated pattern drawings looked abnormal, with pieces interwoven/overlapping. The goal is one large-format plotter pattern, not tiled A3/A4 sheets.
- A 1:1 drawing/PDF exists in Library, but should not be assumed production-safe solely because it exists. Pattern pieces, grainlines, seam allowances, notches, balance points, pocket positions, button position, lapel break/roll, vents, cut quantities and scale must be checked.
- A physical toile is required because posture, shoulder slope, arm pitch and left/right asymmetry cannot be inferred reliably from circumferences alone.
- Fitting order: front/back balance → shoulder slope → collar/neck → chest → lapel roll → waist/side body → sleeve pitch → sleeve length → trouser rise/seat → thigh/knee/taper → hem/break. Do not suppress waist before balance is correct.

---

# 4. Project: Adaptive Buoyancy Recovery Platform / Titanic / Fragile Shipwrecks

## Goal and intent
- User described an original engineering concept for recovering fragile shipwrecks, including the Titanic, called an **adaptive buoyancy recovery platform**.
- The central intent is to investigate whether the idea solves a real engineering problem or represents a meaningful engineering leap.
- Commercial goal: potentially license the research/technology to a buyer and earn royalties, rather than necessarily manufacturing the system personally.
- Desired work: technically honest development plan, rigorous research paper, equations and diagrams, prior-art awareness, and commercialization/licensing package.
- User asked for a PDF deliverable and said they would send the original essay/files when they find them.

## Development principles captured
- Do not claim guaranteed patentability or novelty without a proper prior-art search and qualified patent advice.
- Preserve confidentiality before public disclosure where patent rights matter; document inventorship, dates, design rationale, calculations and variants.
- Develop a clear technical disclosure: problem statement, system architecture, buoyancy/force balance, load paths, structural constraints, control/monitoring concept, deployment/recovery sequence, failure modes, safety factors, test plan, uncertainty analysis and comparison to existing salvage methods.
- Research paper should distinguish established engineering principles from hypotheses and unvalidated concept claims.
- Commercial plan should identify potential licensees/buyers, value proposition, evidence needed for due diligence, IP strategy, staged proof-of-concept, and realistic royalty/licensing structures. No guarantee of sale or royalties.

## Known limitation
- The original full essay/specification is not present in the currently retrieved Library results. The concept summary is available, but detailed mechanism/equations cannot be reconstructed faithfully without the user's original notes. A library search did not surface a dedicated Titanic/shipwreck file in the available inventory.

---

# 5. Project: 206 WRC — Peugeot 206 / Iran

- Project label: **206 WRC**.
- Platform: **IKCO Peugeot 206 four-door sedan**, for Iran.
- Exterior: white; white OZ Rally Racing 4x4 wheels.
- Visual philosophy: keep the car looking relatively stock / silent-look; body changes should be discreet.
- Drivetrain ambition: R32-style all-wheel drive concept.
- Transmission: short-ratio 6-speed manual.
- Engine concept: **EW10J4S twin-charged** (supercharger + turbocharger concept). The intent is a highly engineered dual-mode setup, not just a cosmetic build.
- Operating modes: daily soft/low-consumption mode and a separate race mode.
- Overall intent: usable daily drivability and lower consumption in one mode, high-performance behavior in race mode, with subtle exterior appearance.
- This is a concept memory summary; detailed validated packaging, engine management, thermal, drivetrain, legality and safety engineering remain to be developed. Do not imply that the twin-charged AWD conversion is already engineered or proven.

---

# 6. Fragrance preferences and personal scent profile

## Core scent direction
Very dark, wet, bitter, inky, woody-green; mossy; wet cold forest; seaweed/salt/mineral water; oud; vetiver; sage; cardamom; ambroxan; tobacco; vanilla in restrained amounts. Strong longevity and prominent projection are desired. User dislikes overly sweet/cloying fragrances. Bitter-orange openings are acceptable if not sour. User wants seductive, dark and dangerous character, and likes rose.

## Fragrances mentioned positively / references
- Lalique Encre Noire — major reference/top reference.
- Nasomatto Black Afgano.
- Tom Ford Oud Minerale.
- Bvlgari Man in Black.
- Dior Sauvage Parfum.
- YSL La Nuit de L’Homme Parfum — explicitly corrected by user as a top favorite.
- Tom Ford Noir Extreme Parfum.
- Parfums de Marly Oajan.
- Bvlgari AQVA Atlantique.
- Dior Homme Intense / Dior Homme Parfum.
- Givenchy Gentleman Réserve Privée.
- Aventus Absolu.
- Green Irish Tweed.
- Le Beau Le Parfum / Paradise Garden.
- Black Opium and Jasmin Noir.

## EDRIS fragrance concept
**AFTER THE RAIN / THE BLACK TIE SHIPYARD**: dark aquatic woody amber extrait; rain-soaked forest plus derelict shipyard; cold mineral/ocean/rain air, oakmoss, smoke/campfire, incense, cognac/boozy facets, chocolate; possible clove, cinnamon, honey and ISO E Super. User likes the idea of Aventus Absolu’s appealing facets but wants more wet forest, darkness and seduction.

Earlier architecture study in Iran, then software engineering through the final semester without graduation. - Instruments/interests: guitar, saxophone/clarinet, bass, piano, drums.
- Genres: jazz, blues, rock, metal, psychedelic, progressive, Latin and electronic.
- Desired path once compared to Full Sail-style film directing major + music minor, but less business-heavy and with more theory/performance.

# 9. Current known preferences about how the assistant should work with these projects

- Carry forward prior decisions and context rather than making the user repeat them.
- Distinguish a concept from a validated engineering design.
- Be honest about uncertainties, prior art, manufacturability and technical limits.
- When creating tailoring documents, ensure the drawings are clean, non-overlapping, correctly scaled and usable by a tailor; avoid presenting an unvalidated pattern as ready to cut.
- Keep EDRIS's house identity coherent and avoid reverting to the old “Uncle Ed’s” name as the public brand, while retaining older filenames as historical artifacts.
- For the shipwreck concept, preserve confidentiality and prepare an IP-aware commercialization path; do not promise patentability or a guaranteed buyer.
- For 206 WRC, preserve the twin-charged EW10J4S, AWD concept, short-ratio six-speed, white body/wheels, stock-looking exterior, and separate daily/race modes as the remembered concept.

---

# 10. What is not included / cannot be guaranteed by this dump

- A verbatim transcript of every chat in the account.
- Every message, attachment, image, or deleted/archived conversation.
- Private account data, internal telemetry, hidden system records, or platform-side records inaccessible to the assistant.
- A complete, verified technical specification for the Titanic recovery platform: the original essay/files were not found in the accessible Library inventory.
- Confirmation that every listed historical measurement or design choice is still current; conflicts are noted where known.

For a true account-wide conversation archive, use ChatGPT's own **Settings → Data Controls → Export Data** workflow. That export is separate from this curated project/personality dump.

8. FITTING / TOILE CHECKLIST
 Make a half-canvas or inexpensive toile before cutting the final tuxedo cloth.
 Confirm jacket length on the wearer while standing naturally and while seated.
 Confirm the 4.5 in lapel remains balanced with the shoulder width; keep the peak long rather than widening it further.
 Check collar gap, lapel roll, front balance and button position from front and side.
 Check that the jacket skims the stomach rather than gripping it; remove excess fabric only after posture and balance are corrected.
 Set sleeve pitch and final sleeve length with the actual dress shirt.
 Fit trousers with the intended tuxedo shoes; set final inseam and break only then.
 Check that the trouser crease falls cleanly from the center of the thigh/knee to the shoe.
IMPORTANT: Target finished measurements are starting specifications, not guaranteed final pattern dimensions. A professional tailor
should draft using their own block/drafting system and correct the toile for posture, shoulder slope, arm pitch, balance and fabric behavior.

# EXTRACTED PDF TEXT: 038_UNCLE_EDS_001_COMPLETE_HOUSE_TAILOR_PATTERN_PACKET.pdf

UNCLE ED'S · 001 — ONE OF ONE · By Edris Nazparvar
PAGE 1
 UNCLE ED'S 001
 ONE OF ONE — THE LEGACY TUXEDO
HOUSE IDEOLOGY · FIT · DESIGN · DRAFTING · CUTTING · CONSTRUCTION
This packet consolidates the design and measurement material retrieved from the user's prior tuxedo drafting work and aligns it with
the UNCLE ED'S house philosophy.
Pattern status: The pattern sheets in this packet are a drafting prototype for toile development. They are not a substitute for the tailor's
final block correction. Do not cut final 280–320 gsm wool until a toile is fitted and corrections are transferred.
HOUSE LAW
 The body is nature. The mind is civilization.
 UNCLE ED'S does not decorate the body; it civilizes natural form through proportion, construction, material and craft.
 Death completes the circle: the garment should feel capable of becoming an heirloom, an object that outlives its maker.
 The outside creates intrigue. The inside rewards curiosity.
 Nothing looks accidental. Exterior luxury; internal engineering.
 Cultural path: Mazandaran / Caspian nature → Tehran modernity → Paris → Rome → Los Angeles → New York. Influences are
translated into proportion, geometry, material and construction—not pasted motifs.
001 GARMENT IDENTITY
 UNCLE ED'S 001 — ONE OF ONE — 2026.
 Structured Boxy Hollywood fit: controlled mass, full chest, natural architectural shoulder, controlled waist, long clean line; never
sloppy oversized.
 Psychological target: composed, intelligent, cultivated, mysterious, subtly dangerous; never costume-like.
 Visible language: refined eveningwear. Hidden language: engineered tailoring.
JACKET
 Crossover single-button architecture; one functional aged-silver house-logo button, 25–28 mm.
 Peak lapel, exactly 12.0 cm finished width; high gorge; elongated sharp peak; completely straight outer edge; no bowed/scooped
line.
 Black satin facing, controlled sheen. White formal shirt and large black satin butterfly bow tie.
 Natural but architectural shoulder; high armhole; full canvas; hand-shaped chest; hand-padded lapel; precise sleeve pitch.
 Long double vents; clean jetted exterior pockets; concealed internal phone/passport/wallet/valuables/repair compartments.
 Four functional aged-silver sleeve buttons; 1.5–2 cm shirt cuff visible.
TROUSERS
 High-rise flat front; 4.5 cm finished waistband target, 5 cm maximum.
 Side adjusters, no belt loops; concealed hook-and-bar; jetted back pockets.
 Fuller thigh, controlled taper, minimal break; no visible satin side stripe in the definitive 001 design.
 Front rise 38 cm and back rise 40 cm are the later, more specific supplied values; verify on body/toile.
MATERIAL / INTERIOR
 Deepest black wool, approx. 280–320 g/m², low-to-medium controlled sheen, enough structure for architecture; subtle green depth
only if it reads black.
 Black/deep forest cupro/Bemberg lining with Persian geometric logic, Art Deco geometry, organic branching/circular pathways and
subtle asymmetry.
 Hidden oxblood accents. Aged-silver hardware. No military insignia, shoulder boards, tactical straps or historical uniform symbols.

UNCLE ED'S 001 — FINAL 1:1 DRAFT PATTERN
ONE OF ONE · THE LEGACY TUXEDO · By Edris Nazparvar · 2026
Draft basis: supplied body measurements + established 001 house design.
Scale: 1:1. Seam allowance outline: 0.8 cm shown dashed. Solid line = stitching/drafting line.
IMPORTANT: This is the final calculated draft from the supplied data, but it must be toile-fitted before final cloth.
Lapel: 12.0 cm finished, straight architectural outer edge, high gorge, peak.
Jacket: structured boxy Hollywood, crossover single button, full canvas, high armhole, double vents.
Trousers: high rise, flat front, 4.5 cm waistband target, side adjusters, minimal break.
Verify 10 cm calibration square after printing. Do not use printer 'fit to page'.
Cutting rule: cut toile first; transfer all fitting corrections to the master pattern; then cut final cloth.

 UNCLE ED’S 001 — ONE OF ONE
FINAL TAILORING / MASTER PATTERN SPECIFICATION
By Edris Nazparvar  2026
Purpose. Controlled drafting specification for the final 001 tuxedo. Finished measurements are garment targets; drafting allowances are
added outside them. The master must be drafted through a professional bespoke block rather than by scaling a rectangle or polygon.
Supersession. This final 001 specification replaces the earlier 11.4 cm lapel and trouser-side-stripe draft: lapel is 12 cm, outer edge
straight, and the trouser has no satin stripe.

UNCLE ED’S 001 — ONE OF ONE
Page 2
2. JACKET MASTER SPECIFICATION
Area
Final 001 construction
Silhouette
Structured boxy Hollywood fit; full chest; controlled waist; long clean body; controlled mass.
Front
Crossover single-button architecture; one functional closure; controlled overlap.
Button
25–28 mm aged-silver house logo; slightly low/moderate stance.
Length
78–80 cm finished; exact hem established during balance fitting.
Shoulder
47–48 cm finished; slight extension, natural rather than military.
Armhole
High armhole, clean scye; enough mobility without a low oversized opening.
Chest / waist / seat
112–114 / 106–108 / 116–118 cm finished.
Vents
Double vents, 26–28 cm.
Pockets
Jetted/clean; restrained breast welt; no external utility.
Construction
Full canvas; shaped chest; hand-padded lapel; reinforced stress points.
Sleeve
Two-piece; full bicep; articulated elbow; natural pitch; 4 functional cuff buttons.
Shirt cuff
1.5–2 cm visible.
Lapel
12 cm peak; high controlled gorge; elongated peak; completely straight outer edge.
Facing
Black satin/grosgrain, controlled sheen, full facing.
Collar
Black satin; clean transition into lapel.
Exterior rule
No shoulder boards, straps, tactical webbing, military insignia, decorative utility or uniform cues.
Pattern pieces: left front, right front, side body, back, upper sleeve, under sleeve, collar, lapel/facing, pocket welts/bags, vent
reinforcement and canvas/interlining pieces. Mark grain, balance marks, notches, pocket levels, button position, roll line, break line, lapel
peak and all cut quantities.

UNCLE ED’S 001 — ONE OF ONE
Page 3
3. TROUSER MASTER SPECIFICATION
Area
Final 001 specification
Rise
Front 38 cm / back 40 cm; high natural waist.
Waistband
4.5 cm finished; 5 cm absolute maximum.
Closure
Concealed hook-and-bar; side adjusters; no belt loops.
Front
Flat front; clean crease.
Seat
116–119 cm finished starting target.
Thigh
64–66 cm finished.
Knee
45–46 cm finished.
Calf / ankle
Use 43 / 26 cm body references; draft movement allowance and set opening with shoe.
Length
100 cm outseam reference; final hem with tuxedo shoe.
Break
Minimal controlled slight break; no pooling.
Stripe
NONE — earlier draft superseded.
Pockets
Clean side pockets and rear welt pockets.
4. SHIRT / ACCESSORIES
White fine formal cotton shirt  formal collar  French cuffs  aged-silver studs  large black butterfly bow tie proportioned deliberately to the
12 cm lapel  clean black formal shoe.
5. INTERIOR / MATERIAL
Deepest black wool, approximately 280–320 g/m², low-to-medium controlled sheen, excellent drape and sufficient structure.
Black/deep-forest cupro/Bemberg lining with restrained oxblood hidden accents. Label: UNCLE ED’S / By Edris Nazparvar / 001 / ONE
OF ONE / 2026.
6. DRAFTING CONTROL
Add seam allowances outside finished dimensions. Keep generous toile allowances at shoulder, side body, center back, collar, sleeve
and trouser seat/rise. Mark grainlines, notches, balance points, darts, pocket positions, button position, lapel roll/break, vent ends and cut
quantities. Every piece must read: UNCLE ED’S 001 / piece name / cut quantity / grainline / seam allowance.
7. FITTING ORDER
Correct in this order: front/back balance → shoulder slope → collar/neck → chest → lapel roll → waist/side body → sleeve pitch → sleeve
length → trouser rise/seat → thigh/knee/taper → hem/break. Never waist-suppress before balance is correct.
8. STATUS
FINAL DESIGN SPECIFICATION / BESPOKE MASTER DRAFT. This is the correct production brief and measurement control sheet. A
physical toile is still required before cutting the final cloth because posture, shoulder slope, arm pitch and left/right asymmetry cannot be
inferred reliably from circumference measurements alone.

# EXTRACTED PDF TEXT: 041_UNCLE_EDS_001_TAILOR_MASTER_PATTERN_PACKET.pdf

UNCLE ED'S 001
 ONE OF ONE — THE LEGACY TUXEDO
 TAILOR MASTER PATTERN & CUTTING PACKET
 By Edris Nazparvar · 2026
IMPORTANT: This packet is the definitive design and drafting specification, but it is not yet a mathematically complete 1:1 production
pattern. The tailor must verify the missing body measurements, draft the full-scale paper pattern, and make a toile before cutting the final
cloth.
1. Definitive jacket design
 Structured Boxy Hollywood fit: strong controlled mass, not slim and not oversized.
 Crossover single-button architecture with one functional closure and controlled overlap. Main button: aged silver, 25–28 mm, Uncle Ed’s
precision-machined logo.
 12 cm peak lapel, high gorge, sharp peak, completely straight outer edge. No bowed or scooped lapel line. Black satin facing.
 Architectural natural shoulder, controlled pad, high armhole, full chest, full canvas. Long clean double vents.
 Exterior pockets jetted with no flaps. Interior pockets concealed and functional.
 Sleeve slightly fuller than contemporary slim; articulated elbow; four functional aged-silver cuff buttons; 1.5–2 cm shirt cuff visible.
2. Shirt, bow tie and trousers
 White formal shirt, French cuffs, aged-silver studs. Large black satin butterfly bow tie.
 High-rise flat-front trousers. Finished waistband target 4.5 cm; absolute maximum 5 cm. Side adjusters, no belt loops, concealed
hook-and-bar.
 Fuller thigh, controlled taper, minimal break. No satin side stripe. Jetted back pockets.
3. Currently verified measurements
 Elbow circumference — 35 cm (verify both arms).
 Shoulder point → elbow — 40 cm.
 Front trouser rise — 38 cm (verify).
 Back trouser rise — 40 cm (verify).
 Calf circumference — 43 cm (verify both legs).
 Ankle circumference — 26 cm (verify both legs).
 Outseam — 100 cm (verify with intended shoes and minimal break).
4. Measurements the tailor must take before final jacket drafting
Measurement
Status
Height
VERIFY
Chest circumference
VERIFY
Natural waist circumference
VERIFY

VERIFY
Neck circumference
VERIFY

VERIFY
Across chest
VERIFY
Across back
VERIFY
Back neck → waist
VERIFY

VERIFY

Posture / shoulder asymmetry
FIT CHECK

5. Jacket pattern pieces to draft
 J01 Left front shell
 J02 Right front shell
 J03 Side body / side panel
 J04 Back shell
 J05 Upper sleeve
 J06 Under sleeve
 J07 Collar / neck piece
 J08 Left lapel/facing
 J09 Right lapel/facing
 J10 Front pocket welt components
 J11 Chest pocket welt
 J12 Vent facing/reinforcement
 J13 Complete lining pieces
 J14 Front chest canvas
 J15 Shoulder reinforcement
 J16 Sleeve head / sleeve wadding
 J17 Pocket and stress-point reinforcement
6. Required markings on every pattern piece
 Piece number and name; cut quantity; material ID; grainline; center-front/center-back or fold line; notches and balance marks;
pocket/button locations; seam and hem allowances; lapel roll/break line; construction reference marks.
 Add a 10 cm calibration square to the master drafting sheet and every tiled print page if the pattern is later digitized for A4 output.
7. Lapel drafting law
 Finished width exactly 12.0 cm at the defined measurement point.
 High gorge and sharp peak. Outer edge must remain straight and architectural between defined points.
 Establish button position and lapel break together so the crossover front remains balanced.
 Draft facing separately and verify the finished pressed width remains 12 cm. Check left/right symmetry before cutting.
8. Construction
 Full canvas. Hand-shaped chest. Hand-padded lapels. Controlled shoulder pad and sleeve head, never military-looking. High armhole.
Precise sleeve pitch from actual posture. Reinforce armholes, pocket openings, button attachment, vents, crotch and waistband. Make a
toile and transfer corrections to the master pattern.
9. Trousers pattern
 Front rise recorded 38 cm and back rise 40 cm — verify on body. Waistband finished 4.5 cm target / 5 cm maximum. High natural waist.
Side adjusters, no belt loops. Calf 43 cm and ankle 26 cm recorded — verify both legs. Outseam 100 cm recorded — verify with shoes.
Minimal break.
10. Materials and identity
 Outer: deepest black wool, approximately 280–320 g/m², controlled sheen, excellent drape and enough structure for architecture. Subtle
deep-green depth only if it reads black.
 Lining: black/deep forest cupro or Bemberg with Persian geometric logic, Art Deco geometry and organic branching/circular pathways;
hidden oxblood accents.
 Hardware: aged silver. No military insignia, shoulder boards, tactical straps, decorative ammunition-like hardware or historical uniform
symbols.
 Label: UNCLE ED’S / By Edris Nazparvar / 001 / ONE OF ONE / 2026.

11. Hidden functional system
 Concealed phone, wallet, passport and valuables compartments; pen slot; small emergency tailoring/repair compartment. Nothing
utility-like visible outside.
 Hidden inscriptions: THE BODY IS NATURE. / THE MIND IS CIVILIZATION. / DEATH COMPLETES THE CIRCLE.
12. Cutting order
 1) Re-measure body. 2) Draft jacket and trouser blocks. 3) Convert to 001 design. 4) Draft shell, facing, canvas, lining and sleeve
pieces. 5) Add allowances and markings. 6) Check symmetry and balance. 7) Cut toile. 8) Fit and correct shoulder, chest, waist, sleeve
pitch, jacket length, lapel break and trouser rise. 9) Transfer corrections to master paper pattern. 10) Cut final cloth.
FINAL DESIGN LAW
Nothing looks accidental. The finished garment must read as an exceptionally refined black evening tuxedo. The engineering is
internal; the visible result is luxury tailoring. The outside creates intrigue. The inside rewards curiosity.


---

 .دوش ماجنا ندب تیعضو و سنالاب حالصا زا سپ طقف مرف حالصا ؛دریگن ار نآ و دروخب رس مکش یور دیاب تکاژ  
 .دوش میظنت یعقاو یمسر نهاریپ اب نیتسآ هیواز و دق  
 .دوش نییعت ودیسکات ییاهن شفک اب راولش دق  
 .دنامب هتسویپ و زیمت شفک ات وناز و نار زکرم زا دیاب راولش یوتا طخ  
 اب و دنک مسر ار وگلا شدوخ یوگلا متسیس اب دیاب طایخ .ورپ نودب شرب لباق ییاهن یوگلا هن ،تسا
.دنک حالصا ار هچراپ راتفر و نیتسآ هقلح ،ندب تیعضو ،تشپ ولج سنالاب ،هناش بیش ،لآوت 
 .راولش راون و هقی یارب یکشم نیرگورگ ای نتاس   تام‌همین تام رهاظ اب یکشم مشپ :یداهنشیپ هچراپ

# EXTRACTED PDF TEXT: _EXPANDED_ARCHIVES/044_USER_DATA_DUMP_AND_PROJECT_FILES/Tuxedo_001/UNCLE_EDS_001_COMPLETE_HOUSE_TAILOR_PATTERN_PACKET.pdf

UNCLE ED'S · 001 — ONE OF ONE · By Edris Nazparvar
PAGE 1
 UNCLE ED'S 001
 ONE OF ONE — THE LEGACY TUXEDO
HOUSE IDEOLOGY · FIT · DESIGN · DRAFTING · CUTTING · CONSTRUCTION
This packet consolidates the design and measurement material retrieved from the user's prior tuxedo drafting work and aligns it with
the UNCLE ED'S house philosophy.
Pattern status: The pattern sheets in this packet are a drafting prototype for toile development. They are not a substitute for the tailor's
final block correction. Do not cut final 280–320 gsm wool until a toile is fitted and corrections are transferred.
HOUSE LAW
 The body is nature. The mind is civilization.
 UNCLE ED'S does not decorate the body; it civilizes natural form through proportion, construction, material and craft.
 Death completes the circle: the garment should feel capable of becoming an heirloom, an object that outlives its maker.
 The outside creates intrigue. The inside rewards curiosity.
 Nothing looks accidental. Exterior luxury; internal engineering.
 Cultural path: Mazandaran / Caspian nature → Tehran modernity → Paris → Rome → Los Angeles → New York. Influences are
translated into proportion, geometry, material and construction—not pasted motifs.
001 GARMENT IDENTITY
 UNCLE ED'S 001 — ONE OF ONE — 2026.
 Structured Boxy Hollywood fit: controlled mass, full chest, natural architectural shoulder, controlled waist, long clean line; never
sloppy oversized.
 Psychological target: composed, intelligent, cultivated, mysterious, subtly dangerous; never costume-like.
 Visible language: refined eveningwear. Hidden language: engineered tailoring.
JACKET
 Crossover single-button architecture; one functional aged-silver house-logo button, 25–28 mm.
 Peak lapel, exactly 12.0 cm finished width; high gorge; elongated sharp peak; completely straight outer edge; no bowed/scooped
line.
 Black satin facing, controlled sheen. White formal shirt and large black satin butterfly bow tie.
 Natural but architectural shoulder; high armhole; full canvas; hand-shaped chest; hand-padded lapel; precise sleeve pitch.
 Long double vents; clean jetted exterior pockets; concealed internal phone/passport/wallet/valuables/repair compartments.
 Four functional aged-silver sleeve buttons; 1.5–2 cm shirt cuff visible.
TROUSERS
 High-rise flat front; 4.5 cm finished waistband target, 5 cm maximum.
 Side adjusters, no belt loops; concealed hook-and-bar; jetted back pockets.
 Fuller thigh, controlled taper, minimal break; no visible satin side stripe in the definitive 001 design.
 Front rise 38 cm and back rise 40 cm are the later, more specific supplied values; verify on body/toile.
MATERIAL / INTERIOR
 Deepest black wool, approx. 280–320 g/m², low-to-medium controlled sheen, enough structure for architecture; subtle green depth
only if it reads black.
 Black/deep forest cupro/Bemberg lining with Persian geometric logic, Art Deco geometry, organic branching/circular pathways and
subtle asymmetry.
 Hidden oxblood accents. Aged-silver hardware. No military insignia, shoulder boards, tactical straps or historical uniform symbols.

UNCLE ED'S · 001 — ONE OF ONE · By Edris Nazparvar
PAGE 2
 Label: UNCLE ED'S / By Edris Nazparvar / 001 / ONE OF ONE / 2026. Hidden inscriptions: THE BODY IS NATURE. / THE MIND IS
CIVILIZATION. / DEATH COMPLETES THE CIRCLE.

UNCLE ED'S · 001 — ONE OF ONE · By Edris Nazparvar
PAGE 3

RECONCILIATION NOTES
 The earlier drafting sheet recorded a 35 cm body rise and trouser targets of 103–105 waist, 116–119 seat, 64–66 thigh, 45–46 knee
and 40–42 leg opening. The later 001 packet supersedes the rise with front 38 / back 40 and supersedes the decorative satin side
stripe with no side stripe. Finished targets should be corrected during toile fitting.
 The earlier sheet proposed 11.4 cm (4.5 in) lapels. The definitive 001 design later increased this to 12.0 cm. Use 12.0 cm.
 The earlier sheet proposed 78–80 cm jacket length. The 73 cm figure is retained as a supplied reference, but final 001 length should
be selected during toile fitting for the long, clean silhouette.

UNCLE ED'S · 001 — ONE OF ONE · By Edris Nazparvar
PAGE 4
DRAFTING FORMULAS & FINISHED TARGETS
JACKET FINISHED TARGETS — STARTING BLOCK
 Finished chest: 112–114 cm (105 body + approximately 7–9 cm ease).
 Finished waist: 106–108 cm (101 body + approximately 5–7 cm ease; do not cinch).
 Finished seat/hem: 116–118 cm (112 body + approximately 4–6 cm ease).
 Finished shoulder: 47–48 cm (46 body shoulder with controlled extension).
 Initial finished jacket length: 78–80 cm is the preferred long modern target from the earlier drafting sheet; verify against the supplied
73 cm reference and body proportions during toile.
 Sleeve: draft from shoulder→wrist 70 cm, shoulder→elbow 40 cm and elbow 35 cm; final cuff position must show 1.5–2 cm shirt cuff.
 Double vent length: approximately 26–28 cm starting point, then fit.
PATTERN PIECES
 J01/J02 left and right front shells; J03 side body; J04 back; J05/J06 upper and under sleeves; J07 collar; J08/J09 facings/lapels;
J10/J11 pocket welts; J12 vent reinforcement; J13 lining; J14 chest canvas; J15 shoulder reinforcement; J16 sleeve head; J17 stress
reinforcements.
 Every piece: name, number, cut quantity, grainline, notches, balance marks, seam allowance, hem allowance, fold line, pocket/button
location and material ID.
 Pattern sheet calibration: 100 × 100 mm square must measure exactly 100 × 100 mm after printing. Print at 100% / actual size; never
'fit to page'.

UNCLE ED'S · 001 — ONE OF ONE · By Edris Nazparvar
PAGE 5
JACKET PATTERN — 1:1 DRAFTING PROTOTYPE SHEETS

starting geometry; add seam allowances only after fitting the toile.

Page 1
 UNCLE ED’S 001 — ONE OF ONE
FINAL TAILORING / MASTER PATTERN SPECIFICATION
By Edris Nazparvar  2026
Purpose. Controlled drafting specification for the final 001 tuxedo. Finished measurements are garment targets; drafting allowances are
added outside them. The master must be drafted through a professional bespoke block rather than by scaling a rectangle or polygon.
Supersession. This final 001 specification replaces the earlier 11.4 cm lapel and trouser-side-stripe draft: lapel is 12 cm, outer edge
straight, and the trouser has no satin stripe.

UNCLE ED’S 001 — ONE OF ONE
Page 2
2. JACKET MASTER SPECIFICATION
Area
Final 001 construction
Silhouette
Structured boxy Hollywood fit; full chest; controlled waist; long clean body; controlled mass.
Front
Crossover single-button architecture; one functional closure; controlled overlap.
Button
25–28 mm aged-silver house logo; slightly low/moderate stance.
Length
78–80 cm finished; exact hem established during balance fitting.
Shoulder
47–48 cm finished; slight extension, natural rather than military.
Armhole
High armhole, clean scye; enough mobility without a low oversized opening.
Chest / waist / seat
112–114 / 106–108 / 116–118 cm finished.
Vents
Double vents, 26–28 cm.
Pockets
Jetted/clean; restrained breast welt; no external utility.
Construction
Full canvas; shaped chest; hand-padded lapel; reinforced stress points.
Sleeve
Two-piece; full bicep; articulated elbow; natural pitch; 4 functional cuff buttons.
Shirt cuff
1.5–2 cm visible.
Lapel
12 cm peak; high controlled gorge; elongated peak; completely straight outer edge.
Facing
Black satin/grosgrain, controlled sheen, full facing.
Collar
Black satin; clean transition into lapel.
Exterior rule
No shoulder boards, straps, tactical webbing, military insignia, decorative utility or uniform cues.
Pattern pieces: left front, right front, side body, back, upper sleeve, under sleeve, collar, lapel/facing, pocket welts/bags, vent
reinforcement and canvas/interlining pieces. Mark grain, balance marks, notches, pocket levels, button position, roll line, break line, lapel
peak and all cut quantities.

UNCLE ED’S 001 — ONE OF ONE
Page 3
3. TROUSER MASTER SPECIFICATION
Area
Final 001 specification
Rise
Front 38 cm / back 40 cm; high natural waist.
Waistband
4.5 cm finished; 5 cm absolute maximum.
Closure
Concealed hook-and-bar; side adjusters; no belt loops.
Front
Flat front; clean crease.
Seat
116–119 cm finished starting target.
Thigh
64–66 cm finished.
Knee
45–46 cm finished.
Calf / ankle
Use 43 / 26 cm body references; draft movement allowance and set opening with shoe.
Length
100 cm outseam reference; final hem with tuxedo shoe.
Break
Minimal controlled slight break; no pooling.
Stripe
NONE — earlier draft superseded.
Pockets
Clean side pockets and rear welt pockets.
4. SHIRT / ACCESSORIES
White fine formal cotton shirt  formal collar  French cuffs  aged-silver studs  large black butterfly bow tie proportioned deliberately to the
12 cm lapel  clean black formal shoe.
5. INTERIOR / MATERIAL
Deepest black wool, approximately 280–320 g/m², low-to-medium controlled sheen, excellent drape and sufficient structure.
Black/deep-forest cupro/Bemberg lining with restrained oxblood hidden accents. Label: UNCLE ED’S / By Edris Nazparvar / 001 / ONE
OF ONE / 2026.
6. DRAFTING CONTROL
Add seam allowances outside finished dimensions. Keep generous toile allowances at shoulder, side body, center back, collar, sleeve
and trouser seat/rise. Mark grainlines, notches, balance points, darts, pocket positions, button position, lapel roll/break, vent ends and cut
quantities. Every piece must read: UNCLE ED’S 001 / piece name / cut quantity / grainline / seam allowance.
7. FITTING ORDER
Correct in this order: front/back balance → shoulder slope → collar/neck → chest → lapel roll → waist/side body → sleeve pitch → sleeve
length → trouser rise/seat → thigh/knee/taper → hem/break. Never waist-suppress before balance is correct.
8. STATUS
FINAL DESIGN SPECIFICATION / BESPOKE MASTER DRAFT. This is the correct production brief and measurement control sheet. A
physical toile is still required before cutting the final cloth because posture, shoulder slope, arm pitch and left/right asymmetry cannot be
inferred reliably from circumference measurements alone.

 لآوت و ورپ تسیل‌کچ .۸
 .دوش هتخود لاقتم ای نازرا هچراپ اب لآوت کی ،یلصا هچراپ شرب زا لبق  
 .دوش یسررب هتسشن و هداتسیا تلاح رد تکاژ دق  
 .دوش یحارط رت‌هدیشک و رتدنلب کیپ کون ،هقی ندرکرت‌نهپ یاج‌هب ؛دشاب لداعتم هناش ضرع اب هقی یچنیا ۵٫۴ ضرع  
 .دوش لرتنک خر‌مین و ور‌هبور زا همکد لحم و ولج سنالاب ،هقی لور ،پگ رلاک  
 .دوش ماجنا ندب تیعضو و سنالاب حالصا زا سپ طقف مرف حالصا ؛دریگن ار نآ و دروخب رس مکش یور دیاب تکاژ  
 .دوش میظنت یعقاو یمسر نهاریپ اب نیتسآ هیواز و دق  
 .دوش نییعت ودیسکات ییاهن شفک اب راولش دق  
 .دنامب هتسویپ و زیمت شفک ات وناز و نار زکرم زا دیاب راولش یوتا طخ  
 اب و دنک مسر ار وگلا شدوخ یوگلا متسیس اب دیاب طایخ .ورپ نودب شرب لباق ییاهن یوگلا هن ،تسا
